- Codec (share.js, ST-142): encode floor-clamping (neg/NaN/undefined -> seed 0, score 0, wave 1, kills 0); decode rejects body tamper, checksum tamper, 5/7-field strings, non-string/empty/upper-case; custom-magic round-trip + cross-magic rejection both directions; 0xffffffff seed + 0x7fffffff score/wave/kills round-trip; checksum always exactly 2 zero-padded base36 chars (padding observed within 200 seeds); 25 distinct seeds -> 25 distinct valid codes all decoding back.
- Stats storage (storage.js): tie ranking counts strictly-greater entries only; full board + lower score -> rank 0, board unchanged; non-finite fields sanitized (wave->1, kills->0) and score <= 0 ignored; newBest only at rank 1; unknown partial keys discarded; invalid fx ('medium') resets to the default tier rather than preserving the earlier 'low'; written payload exactly {version, ...clean} with the known keys; corrupt daily payload -> null (invalid JSON) / {key:'',value:0} (empty v1); recordDaily same-day raise vs new-day rollover; getBest/setBest non-finite/non-numeric/negative -> 0.
- Hotkey contract (F, ST-157): toggleFxQuality parity over 9 flips + defensive non-'low' -> 'low'; setSettings({fx:'low'}) persists to the raw store, survives a simulated reload, and leaves volume/sfx/reducedMotion untouched.
- Tests: 491/491 (was 470; 21 new, 0 failures). Build: exit 0. No rendering change - no browser smoke (pure core, node --test only).
- Result: DONE. ST-162 closed. Next I163 ST-163 browser verify E8D (all screens + screenshots + console clean).

## I165 - UX fix round: menu status row + Enter focus guard (ST-165)
- Goal: ST-165 acceptance - UX fix round over the E8D surface (fixes, browser-verified). Skill: cgs-ux-review.
- UX review (cgs-ux-review contract):
  - Friction: (a) I164 gate finding - the MENU showed no live mute/fx state (only the pause screen had the M/F rows), so a keyboard user could not see the effect of M/F without pausing; (b) NEW - global Enter vs focused buttons: Enter on a focused DAILY RUN button triggered both the native click (startRun daily) and the global startRun(normal), with the global handler running last and silently overriding the button the user targeted; the same class of bug hit the game-over COPY RESULT button (Enter restarted the run instead of copying the code).
  - Comprehension: the DAILY button and COPY RESULT button now do exactly what their labels say, for both mouse and keyboard; the menu status row uses the exact phrasing of the pause rows ('M - MUTE (x)' / 'F - FX (y)') so the two screens read identically.
  - Accessibility: keyboard operability improved - focused buttons are activated by Enter as every user expects (native behavior preserved; the global handler yields instead of fighting it). The status row is static text (no motion FX, reduced-motion safe by construction) and scales with --vr-fs. Mouse flow unchanged (all tappable buttons blur() after click, so body focus is restored and global Enter still works).
  - UX verdict: both findings fixed, no new friction introduced; remaining E8D surface (tutorial, kill feed, banner, share flow) re-reviewed, no further blocking items found for this round.
- Fix 1 (screens.js): menuStatus row - 'const menuStatus = line(menu, ...)' inserted after the menu grid; updated in the MENU branch of update() via set() from screenOpts.muted/screenOpts.fx (already passed per frame by main.js, no main.js change needed for this fix).
- Fix 2 (main.js): Enter branch computes onButton = document.activeElement.tagName === 'BUTTON' and skips the global start/restart/resume when true (native button activation owns Enter).
- Verification (browser ?nocache=165/165b/165c/165d):
  - Menu row: default 'M - MUTE (OFF)   F - FX (HIGH)'; after M+F -> 'M - MUTE (ON)   F - FX (LOW)' with the pause-screen labels showing the same values (parity); restored to defaults; fontScale 1.5 -> computed 18px (12px at scale 1); screenshot .evidence/I165/I165_1_menu_status.png.
  - Enter guard: real DAILY button click -> seed 317547475 (the documented 2026-11-10 daily seed, matches I147); fresh tab + focused DAILY + physical Enter -> seed 317547475 (daily run, bug fixed); fresh tab + body focus + Enter -> seed 17035 (normal run, no regression); game over + focused COPY RESULT + Enter -> 'COPIED!' flash observed by a 50ms text poller, state stayed GAME_OVER (no restart).
- Tests: 491/491 (unchanged - render/boot layer only, no core diff). Build: exit 0. Console 0/0 (one info-level adaptive-quality notice from background-tab throttling).

- Skill: cgs-localize (contract: Summary / String Context / Text Expansion / Locale Risk / Review / Blocking issues / Changed or proposed files / Verification evidence / Next owner).

- Inventory result: ~95 curated player-facing strings:
  - screens.js: title (brand, untranslated), tagline, touch/fine keycap rows, TOP 5, TAP/PRESS ENTER TO START (+RESTART pair), DAILY RUN + aria, best/daily dynamic lines, I165 status row 'M - MUTE (x)   F - FX (y)', PAUSED + RESUME/RESTART/QUIT, GAME OVER + SCORE/BEST/NEW BEST!/BOARD RANK #n/KILLS n WAVE n/MAX COMBO n TIME m:ss/ACCURACY n% SHOTS h/f, daily badge ('DAILY RUN - key  NEW DAILY BEST'), COPY RESULT/COPIED!/COPY FAILED + aria + caption, '- no scores yet -' empty state.
  - hud.js: 6 power-up tags (RAPID/SPREAD/SHIELD/MAGNET/OVERDRIVE/SLOW-MO), 3 mod tags (FAST ROCKS/SMALL ROCKS/INWARD DRIFT), COMBO, WAVE n, BEST n.
  - waveBanner.js: WAVE n, CLEARED, ENDLESS, MOD_LABELS (shared family with hud), THE SPLICER/THE MONOLITH + 'WAVE n - maxHp HP' sub, NEXT WAVE IN n.
  - killFeed.js: THE SPLICER/THE MONOLITH, WARDEN, MINE, SEEKER, GOLDEN ROCK, L/M/S ROCK.
  - tutorialOverlay.js: HOW TO PLAY, sub pair (fly, auto-fire... / fly, aim, fire...), keycap rows, 'survive the waves - bosses arrive on wave 5 and beyond' (widest string), TAP TO LAUNCH/LAUNCH, ENTER, dismiss aria.
  - settingsPanel.js: AUDIO card (VOLUME/SFX/MUSIC + arias), VISUALS card (FX QUALITY LOW/HIGH, REDUCED MOTION, FONT SCALE NORMAL/LARGE, SHAPE CUES, COLORBLIND, SLOW-MO HINT + arias), footer 'TAB NAVIGATES - ARROWS MOVE - SPACE TOGGLES'.
  - bossHealth.js: SPLICER / MONOLITH bar labels.
  - main.js: FX AUTO-SET TO LOW (LOW FPS), FX HIGH/LOW toast, SPLICER INBOUND, WARDEN INBOUND, WEAPON LEVEL n, context-loss message + RELOAD GAME.
- Excluded as non-localizable: keycap hardware glyphs (WASD/MOUSE/ENTER/DRAG/2 FINGERS...), audio.js (0 UI strings), debug overlay, SFX cue names, storage keys.
- Key naming convention (proposed): <area>.<item>[_<variant>] snake_case - menu.*, pause.*, over.*, hud.*, banner.*, feed.*, tutorial.*, settings.*, toast.*, ctxlost.*, keycaps.* (~70 keys). Dynamic parts become {var} placeholders, never sprintf %s.
- Extraction plan (future pass, NOT executed): src/core/strings.js DOM-free (core contract preserved) with en table + t(key,vars) (throws on unknown key in dev) + kc(keycap); render modules swap ~95 textContent literals; tests/strings.test.js (every key resolves, placeholders match call sites, unknown key throws); ordering screens -> settingsPanel -> banner/killfeed/hud -> tutorial/toasts/ctxlost, one commit per group.
- Text expansion: widest = settings footer hint (44ch, wraps OK), tutorial boss note (58ch, card has no overflow:hidden), copy caption; keycap grids - captions wrap (no nowrap), touch column '2 FINGERS / pause' is the squeeze point; banner 40px/52px letter-spacing 8px; kill feed right-aligned ~18ch ceiling - keep localized rock terms short.
- Pluralization: none needed - all counts number-suffixed; board rank numeric (no ordinals). Only latent spot: over.rank if a locale ever wants ordinals.
- Locale risk (7): (1) boss names are proper nouns - RECOMMENDATION keep English in all locales, translate surrounding grammar only (creative sign-off W1); (2) m:ss time kept as raw pair; (3) n% percent placement needs number/% as separate tokens; (4) SHOTS h/f as {hit}/{fired} tokens, locale picks order; (5) RTL not a target (flex layouts would contain the damage); (6) CJK falls back to system fonts (accepted risk); (7) daily key ISO date already locale-neutral.
- Blockers: none. Warnings: W1 boss-name translation policy (creative-director lane), W2 keycap-per-layout mapping is a separate workstream (this doc defines only the keycaps.* seam).
- CUTTABLE confirmed: deleting this story costs nothing at runtime; the game is fully playable and tested without it.
- Verification: docs-only - no rendering change, no browser smoke required. Quality bar rerun: npm test 491/491 (unchanged), node scripts/build.mjs exit 0.

## I167 - UI perf check (text drawing) (ST-167)

- Skill: cgs-perf-profile (contract: Summary / Metric / Budget / Bottleneck / Optimization / Blocking issues / Changed or proposed files / Verification evidence / Next owner).
- Method: live Chromium (Playwright) on the dev build; deterministic 60Hz drive (frozen rAF stub + __vr.advance(1/60) xN with per-tick microtask yields to flush MutationObserver per tick; the tab's rAF was environmentally throttled to ~1Hz, so real-time sampling was unusable); MutationObserver on document.body {subtree, childList, attributes, characterData}; static audit for forced-reflow reads across src/render/*.js.
- Finding: waveBanner.js sync() wrote the banner big-line nodeValue + sub textContent every frame of the 1.8-2.2s animations, committing 3 DOM mutations/frame during the hold phase (402 / 479 / 294 per wave / boss / cleared animation). This was the only recurring non-essential writer in the whole HUD tree - everything else (hud.js set() caches, kill feed quantized steps, display/classList toggles) was measured at 0 committed mutations or already change-guarded.
- Fix: lastBig / lastSub write-on-change caches in sync() (matching the existing HUD set() pattern), reset in the end-reset branch. Measured: wave 402->84 (-79%), boss 479->204 (-57%), hold 3/tick -> 0/tick; definitive 60-tick log shows the only mutation target is #wave-banner style, all inside the first 24 ticks (one-time text writes + opacity/transform entry punch).
- Budgets: steady UI text 0 mutations/tick; worst burst tick 54 (6-rock kill: 5 feed rows + popups + score + HUD, ~10 small nodes, ~0.5ms main-thread); all animated props opacity/transform (compositor-friendly); zero layout-triggering reads (static audit); will-change:auto (no permanent promotion of burst-animated banners).
- Not changed (measured no-ops, documented why): kill feed hold opacity writes (browser short-circuits same-value style writes), HUD (already set()-cached), display/classList toggles.
- Residual note: one early pre-fix run showed a mislabeled attributes@DIV target at ~1.4/tick; the definitive clean-state full log proves no other attribute target exists - probe label artifact, not a real source.
- Doc: production/perf-ui-text.md. Changed: src/render/waveBanner.js + doc.
- Quality bar: npm test 491/491 (render-layer fix, no core diff), node scripts/build.mjs exit 0. Browser smoke (?nocache=167c): intermission countdown renders, WAVE 1 banner correct (screenshot .evidence/I167/I167_1_wave_banner_smoke.png), console 0 errors / 0 warnings, NaN scan (ship.pos, scoreKeeper, rock pos) 0 non-finite.
- Result: DONE. ST-167 closed. Next I168 ST-168 E8D docs + changelog (E8D closeout).

## I170 - Music intensity layers (ST-170)
- Story: ST-170 | Goal: wire I169's musicIntensity(x) seam from world state (wave ramp + boss floor) so the drone bed escalates through a run.
- Design: x = waveCeil*(1-exp(-max(0,wave-1)/waveK)), bossFloor while a boss is alive, clamp [0,1]. Grounded in the shipped difficulty curve: boss.everyWave=5 (bossFloor covers every boss window); speedMult=min(2.1, 1+0.06*wave) caps at wave 19, ramp at 0.568/0.583 by waves 18/19, plateaus to waveCeil 0.75 - the boss step (0.9) reads as a step-up, not a continuation; wave 1 = exactly 0, wave 2 = 0.060. Tunables CONFIG.music.intensity {waveK:12, waveCeil:0.75, bossFloor:0.9}.
- Changed: src/core/musicIntensity.js (NEW, pure, DOM-free); src/core/config.js (CONFIG.music.intensity); tests/musicIntensity.test.js (NEW, 8 tests); src/main.js (syncVisuals wiring: PLAYING-gated, change-cached |dx|>0.02 - I167 discipline, reset off-PLAYING; +__vr.musicIntensityVal probe); design/audio-music.md (ST-170 implementation section per cgs-team-audio contract: Audio Goal / Mapping / Tunables / Wiring / Mix Target / QA / Risks / Verification).
- Verification: parse node --check clean (main.js via .mjs copy; config.js imports OK, CONFIG.music.intensity verified); 499/499 (491 prior + 8 new); build 0; browser smoke ?nocache=170 Enter: baseline PLAYING wave 1 __vr.musicIntensityVal() 0 -> __vr.advance(50) wave 2 0.059967 (= 0.75*(1-e^(-1/12)) exact; target ROSE = wiring live); console 0 errors/0 warnings; NaN scan (ship + 10 rocks + warden + boss refs) 0; screenshot .evidence/I170/I170_1_playing.png.
- Result: DONE. ST-170 closed.
- Risk/follow-up: change-cache hysteresis 0.02 only coalesces pushes (waves only ever advance); if waveK is later tuned low, mid-run waves may feel flat (the boss step is still guaranteed). Next I171 ST-171 music toggle (hotkey + options row).

## I172 - Bullet-time audio (ST-172)
- Story: ST-172 | Goal: aural treatment for the slow-mo power-up - while the world is dilated the music bed drops in pitch + darkens, then glides back (Max-Payne-lineage bullet-time cue), grounded in YouTube research (Eakins FMOD bullet-time, Aftertouch slow-mo SFX, Tom Player Music "Bullet time"; transcripts rate-limited, Coverage note in design/audio-music.md).
- Mapping (src/core/timeWarp.js, NEW, pure, DOM-free): timeWarpX(scale) = clamp(1 - scale, 0, 1) on the game seam world.enemyScale() (1 normally, 0.4 while slow-mo is active - CONFIG.powerup.types.slowmo) => slow-mo lands at x = 0.6; timeWarpCents(x, {centsMax: 700}) = -x*centsMax => exactly -420 cents at slow-mo (a bit over a major third; the proportional -1586 rejected as sub-bass - real cues drop by semitones/thirds). x = 0 returns explicit 0, not -0 (assert/strict uses Object.is).
- Tunables: CONFIG.music.warp {centsMax: 700, filterKeep: 0.3} (src/core/config.js).
- Render (src/render/audio.js): audio.timeWarp(x, {centsMax, filterKeep}) - detunes pad osc + sub drone via o.detune (separate AudioParam: composes with the I169 18s chord freq glides); pluck inherits current cents at creation; lowpass retarget = (1200 + 1400*intensity) * (1 - x*(1-keep)) (composes with the I170 intensity opening); all setTargetAtTime(tau 0.25). No new nodes or timers - menu-safe no-op while the bed is stopped.
- Wiring (src/main.js syncVisuals): PLAYING-gated wx = timeWarpX(world.enemyScale()); change-cached |dx| > 0.02 (I167/I170 discipline - no per-frame AudioParam churn); _timeWarp resets to 0 off-PLAYING so a (re)start re-applies from rest; +2 __vr probes (timeWarp, warpCents).
- Seam correction: handoff referenced world.game.enemyScale(), but world.game is the GameState machine; the actual method is World.enemyScale() (src/core/game.js) - wiring + comments fixed to world.enemyScale() before smoke.
- Changed: src/core/timeWarp.js (NEW); tests/timeWarp.test.js (NEW, 7 tests); src/core/config.js (CONFIG.music.warp); src/render/audio.js (musicWarpCents state + reset, pluck inherit, timeWarp(), export); src/main.js (import, _timeWarp cache, syncVisuals seam, 2 probes); design/audio-music.md (ST-172 implementation section per cgs-team-audio contract).
- Verification: parse node --check clean (audio.js + main.js via .mjs copies; config.js imports, CONFIG.music.warp verified); 510/510 (503 + 7 new); build 0; browser smoke ?nocache=172: PLAYING (ship invulnerable), baseline warpCents 0 -> world.powerups.apply('slowmo') -> enemyScale 0.4 -> next frame warpCents exactly -420 -> advance(2s) still -420 (3.0s left, active) -> advance(4s) warpCents 0, enemyScale 1, inactive; console 0 errors/0 warnings; NaN scan (ship + 5 rocks + velocity) 0; screenshot .evidence/I172/I172_1_slowmo_active.png (SLOW-MO HUD chip + tint + hint visible).
- Result: DONE. ST-172 closed.
- Risk/follow-up: I174 (death slow-mo) can reuse the timeWarp seam - main.js will call audio.timeWarp with a death scale for ~0.8s. Next I173 ST-173 SFX pass for new content (bomb, drone, mini-boss, pickup).

## I173 - SFX pass for new content (ST-173)
- Story: ST-173 | Goal: SFX pass for new content - the drone, the Warden mini-boss and the pickups were silently re-using cues from older content; give each class its own voice sized to its tier (cgs-team-audio contract: Audio Goal / Mapping / Tunables / Wiring / Mix Target / QA / Research / Verification).
- Audit: ALL events were already mapped in SFX_CUES (main.js) - no missing events; the problem was reuse: droneDecay (I129) -> drone (full seeker zap), wardenSpawn (ST-136/137) -> bossSpawn (full 3-note tritone stinger), wardenDeath -> bossDeath (impact-tier boom), all 7 pickup types (rapid/spread/shield/magnet/bomb/overdrive/slowmo) -> one powerup ding (overdrive already had its own override).
- Mapping: 4 new CUES in src/render/audio.js (house-style inline specs, no CONFIG additions): droneDecay sine 340->140 0.18s g0.14 min 0.1 no noise (battery fizzle, background tier); wardenSpawn 2-note low sting [[55,41,0.5,0,0.26],[82.4,79,0.4,0.1,0.18]] g0.22 min 0.4 noise 0.25 (compact variant of bossSpawn - 2 of 3 notes, 0.08 lower gain); wardenDeath square 70->32 0.45s g0.34 min 0.2 noise 0.3 (mid boom, below the 0.45 impact tier); slowmo sine 880->1320 0.2s g0.18 min 0.05 (brighter + quieter than the plain 660->990 ding - cold shimmer for the ice-blue ST-115 drop). Bomb cue verified only: already its own impact-tier cue (sine 90->40, 0.5s, g0.55, noise 0.4) - no change.
- Wiring (src/main.js): SFX_CUES droneDecay 'drone'->'droneDecay', wardenSpawn 'bossSpawn'->'wardenSpawn', wardenDeath 'bossDeath'->'wardenDeath' (wardenArmor + beltHit keep shieldHit); dispatch ternary extended: powerup events resolve slowmo->'slowmo', overdrive->'overdrive' (unchanged), else->'powerup' (unchanged).
- Research (YouTube; metadata/chapters verified, transcripts rate-limited - Coverage note in design/audio-music.md): Tender Rondo 'Special Infected Spawn Cues' (Valve, youtu.be/pUn8HNpiR3w, 2.7M views; countdown/respawn/end stinger chapters) - spawn cues are distinct PER enemy class, grounds separate wardenSpawn vs bossSpawn; Sound Around 'Dramatic Dun Dun Dun... Classic Suspense Audio Stinger' (youtu.be/W6DEnw-h1pw, 25K views) - short low tension hit for spawns, grounds the low 2-note sting over a bright arpeggio; Free Video Sound Effects HD 'Epic Retro Game Power-Up Sound' (youtu.be/08UkjjAq7Rg) + Creator Assets '8-Bit Powerup Sound Effects' (youtu.be/SoZhpnTuQBo, 590K views) + Nyonyxx 'How to Make: 8bit Power up sound' (youtu.be/1kVkyN5ybdQ, 3:33) - pickup = short rising bright ding/arpeggio, grounds slowmo as a brighter shorter ding variant.

## I174 - Death slow-mo (ST-174)
- Story: ST-174 | Goal: death slow-mo - every ship hit dilates the audio for a 0.8s death-beat window (bullet-time), reusing the I172 timeWarp seam; audio-only (world-sim dilation scoped out) (cgs-team-audio contract: Audio Goal / Mapping / Tunables / Wiring / Mix Target / QA / Research / Verification).
- Core: src/core/deathWarp.js (new, pure, DOM-free): deathWarpX(remain, dur, deathScale=0.25) = (1-deathScale)*clamp(remain/dur, 0, 1) - the exact composition of timeWarpX (implied scale 1-(1-deathScale)*p; proven by test). dur <= 0 -> 0.
- Tunables: CONFIG.music.warp.death = { scale: 0.25, dur: 0.8 } (in the existing warp block). Peak = x0.75 time = -525 cents at the hit frame; linear decay to 0 over 0.8s. No new audio nodes or timers.
- Wiring (src/main.js): _deathWarpT (seconds left) armed on hit in the state-always-advances chain (state-agnostic like the vortex chain - it is audio, not motion, so it works with reduced motion); decayed in syncVisuals; warp line wx = max(timeWarpX(enemyScale), death window) with the 0.02 hysteresis + forced exact push on window close; reset off-PLAYING. Probe: __vr.deathWarpT().
- Fix (found in the first smoke): stale sub-hysteresis tail - the 0.02 change-cache never pushed the final sub-threshold step of the linear decay; warpCents() sat stale at -10.9375 (should be 0) and -426.56 (should be -420 with the power-up active). _deathClosed flag forces one exact push when the window closes. Lesson: any change-cached retarget of a decaying value needs a forced exact push at the end.
- Research (YouTube; metadata/chapters verified, transcripts rate-limited - Coverage note in design/audio-music.md): Rockstar Games 'Max Payne 3 Design and Technology Series: Bullet Time' (youtu.be/td7Y6ISS3nk, 312K views, 3:08) - bullet time = dilation to even the odds; HeavynSent 'The Influence Of Bullet Time In Gaming' (youtu.be/gbhuqM098Yk, 6.5K views, 21:35) - slow-mo as the dramatic death beat; HoumanRaps 'Sandevistan in Real Time' (youtu.be/Hn1UpCKrepo, 1.4M views) - short self-terminating windows with their own aural identity; 1up Indie 'Freeze/bullet time effect [GameMaker Studio 2 | Vfx]' (youtu.be/BSzl5aFMddo, 2.4K views; chapters 0:00 preview / 0:37 desaturate + fx layer) - the visual freeze recipe, deferred to ST-176 (damage vignette).
- Verification: 8 unit tests (tests/deathWarp.test.js): peak 0.75, explicit 0 via Object.is at remain 0, linear mid-values, monotonicity, clamps (remain>dur, remain<0), degenerate (dur<=0, deathScale=1), custom deathScale with <1e-12 float tolerance (1-0.9 is not binary-exact), exact composition vs timeWarpX. Parse node --check clean (main.js via .mjs copy). 518/518 (+8). Build 0. Browser smoke ?nocache=174c (real Enter keypress -> PLAYING, audio ctx running, ship invulnerable + settle loop): baseline warpCents exactly 0; A emit hit + advance(0.05) -> deathWarpT exactly 0.783333, cents -514.0625; B 55 advances -> window 0, cents exactly 0 (stale tail gone); C1 slow-mo power-up -> -420; C2 hit on top -> -514.0625 (deeper wins); D1 12 advances -> -426.56 (quantized handoff, within 10 of -420); D2 35 advances to close -> exactly -420 with slow-mo still active (forced close push); E 52 advances until slow-mo expires -> cents 0 (zero drift at idle). Console 0 errors/0 warnings; NaN scan (ship + 6 rocks) 0; screenshot .evidence/I174/I174_1_death_slowmo.png.
- Result: DONE. ST-174 closed.
- Risk/follow-up: none material - audio-only path, no sim change; the max() composition means power-up and death warps never fight (deeper wins, exact handback on close). Next I175 ST-175 shake table.

## I175 - Unified screen-shake table (ST-175)

- Tuning: CONFIG.fx.shakeTable replaces shakeHit (0.5) + shakeBomb (1.0) with 8 rows: hit 0.5, bomb 1.0, mineExplode 0.4, droneExplode 0.35, bossSpawn 0.7, bossDeath 1.0, wardenSpawn 0.5, wardenDeath 0.8 (the exact pre-ST-175 values - six hardcoded literals + two config values). Envelope unchanged (master 1, decay 6, amplitude 0.5).

- Wiring (src/main.js): the 8 juice call sites now use shakeEnergy(event, CONFIG.fx.shakeTable); probes __vr.shakeEnergy(type) and __vr.shakeMag().
- Verification: 9 unit tests (tests/shakeTable.test.js): exact key set, value preservation vs pre-ST-175 call-site values, (0,1] range, unknown -> 0, invalid table/entry -> 0 without throwing, explicit-0 entry, createShake integration (max-stacking, master scale), master 0 no-op. Console 0 errors / 0 warnings; NaN scan (ship + 6 rocks) 0; screenshot .evidence/I175/I175_1_shake_table.png.

## I177 - Pickup burst + magnet trails (ST-177)
- Story: ST-177 | Goal: pickup burst + magnet trails (cgs-team-combat contract) - every pickup pops a color-coded sparkle in its own orb color, and an active MAGNET shows the orb streaking toward the ship with a comet wisp; both motion-gated.

Pickup burst count max(8, ceil(fxCount*0.5)) (=24 at FX high), radial, speed 7, life 0.5; overdrive keeps its byte-identical fountain (fxCount, speed 8, life 0.5) + flash. Magnet trail: 2 particles, magnet green, speed 1.5, life 0.35 (the orb accelerates ahead of the dragged particles into a tail; cost tiny - maxActiveDrops = 1).
- Core: src/core/powerups.js - drop.magnetPull per-step boolean (this.active('magnet') && dist < magnetRadius && dist > 0.001), single source of truth; physics unchanged (the same condition now also gates the pull).
- Wiring (src/main.js): a new else-if (e.type === 'powerup') branch in the if (!motionOff) juice chain fires the sparkle (placed after the overdrive branch, so overdrive stays byte-identical); a per-frame loop in syncVisuals (gated !motionOff && PLAYING) bursts at each drop with magnetPull; probes __vr.particleLive() + __vr.magnetPullCount().
- Verification: +1 unit test (tests/powerups.test.js): magnetPull false with no magnet; true only when magnet active AND in-radius (3<7 true, 9>7 false). Parse node --check clean (main.js via .mjs, config.js, powerups.js, test). 538/538 (+1). Build 0. Part 2 ?nocache=177b (reducedMotion true seeded, reload, Enter): baseline 0; powerup(rapid)+advance -> live stays 0 (juice motion-gated); magnet+in-radius drop+advance -> magnetPullCount 1 (core flag motion-independent) but trail live stays 0 (trail loop motion-gated). Chose the minimal canonical juice; no new asset/shader.

The motion-independent toast + levelUp SFX cue are untouched.

Part 2 ?nocache=178b (reducedMotion: true seeded, reload, Enter): emit weaponLevelUp(L2) + advance -> fan stays 0 and live stays 0 (fanfare motion-gated) but tally +1 (toast + levelUp SFX still fire - the motion-independent halves). Console 0 errors / 0 warnings both parts; NaN scan (ship pos/vel + drops) 0; screenshot .evidence/I178/I178_1_weapon_upgrade_fanfare.png (PLAYING, WAVE 1, "WEAPON LEVEL 3" toast).

Chose the minimal canonical juice; tier-colored to match the existing HUD/bullet colors; no new asset/shader.

## I179 - Feel balance pass E8-3: unified flash-bump table (ST-179)
- Story: ST-179 | Goal: balance pass E8-3 (feel params) (cgs-balance-check contract) - centralize the 9 scattered inline flashA bumps into one data-driven table (mirroring the ST-175 shakeTable move) and produce a calibration audit of the full feel ladder.
- Balance Lever: new CONFIG.fx.flashTable { hit 0.5, bossDeath 0.5, bossSpawn 0.4, wardenDeath 0.4, bomb 0.35, mineExplode 0.25, wardenSpawn 0.25, droneExplode 0.2, overdrive 0.15 } + new pure core src/core/flashTable.js (flashEnergy(type, table); 0 for unmapped/invalid). weaponLevelUp excluded (already config-driven via weaponFanfare.flash 0.2 - documented). Behavior-preserving: values unchanged from the 9 pre-ST-179 inline literals (proven by test).
- Exploit Risk: none - flashA is a visual accumulator capped at 0.8, not a gameplay resource; no input path reads it. Centralizing 9 magic numbers into a data-driven table is the AGENTS.md "all tunables in config.js, data-driven" principle applied to flash.
- Pacing: the audit (design/feel-balance.md) maps the 5 feel channels (shake / flash / punch / hit-stop / fov) per event. Key finding: the channels are intentionally decoupled BY MEANING - shake = physical force/blast radius, flash = personal urgency/spectacle, punch = weight, hit-stop/fov = impact scale. Cross-channel mismatches (bomb top-shake/mid-flash; hit mid-shake/top-flash) are deliberate, not bugs. Verdict: NO value changes - every value is matched to its channel role (grounded: match the juice to the hit, do not turn everything up). 3 borderline watch-items flagged for I183 (feel tuning 2, playtest-driven).
- Telemetry Need: a per-run juice-event counter + a subjective feel survey (or a proxy: time-to-next-input after a hit) so I183 is data-driven instead of guess-tuned.
- Wiring (src/main.js): import flashEnergy; 9 juice-dispatch call sites now read the table (full-line-anchored; the 0.25/0.4/0.5 duplicates disambiguated by the event line); probe __vr.flashEnergy(t).
- Verification: +9 unit tests (tests/flashTable.test.js, mirrors shakeTable.test.js): exact 9-key set, value preservation vs pre-ST-179 literals, (0,0.8] range, unknown/excluded -> 0 (weaponLevelUp), invalid table/entry -> 0 without throwing, explicit-0 entry, 0.8-cap over-charge composition, top-two stack > cap. Parse node --check clean (flashTable.js, config.js, main.js via .mjs, test). 547/547 (+9). Build 0. Browser smoke ?nocache=179a (real Enter -> PLAYING, ship invulnerable): flashEnergy probe == all 9 table values exactly, unknown + weaponLevelUp == 0 (wiring live); emit hit + advance(0.05) -> shakeMag 0.37040911 = 0.5*exp(-6*0.05) exactly (the hit branch, which carries the new flashEnergy line, executes clean); eventTally.hit +1; 2D finite scan (ship x/y + rocks + drops) 0 bad. Console 0 errors / 0 warnings; screenshot .evidence/I179/I179_1_flash_table_probe.png (PLAYING).
- Research: YouTube (metadata; transcripts rate-limited - Coverage note): GDevelop "How To Do Game Feel Right" (hitflash + screen-shake are separate juice channels), Vlambeer Jan Willem Nijman "The Art of Screenshake" (shake communicates mass/impact; overuse = meaningless/nauseating), "Your game works. Why does it feel dead?" (doctrine: match the juice to the hit, do not turn everything up). Applied to justify the decoupled-channel verdict.
- Result: DONE. ST-179 closed.
- Risk/follow-up: behavior-preserving (values unchanged; value preservation proven by the 9 unit tests, wiring by the probe). No playtest was done, so the no-changes verdict is a calibration REASONING + grounding call, not a measured one - the 3 watch-items are I183 playtest-driven inputs. Next I180 ST-180 audio headless tests (WebAudio stub).

## I180 - Audio headless test suite: WebAudio stub + 18 tests (ST-180)
- Story: ST-180 | Goal: headless unit tests for src/render/audio.js (acceptance: 10+ new tests, node --test) - cgs-test-setup contract (fixture + harness + repeatable validation loop + evidence).
- Summary: the 26-cue WebAudio synth layer had zero automated coverage; it now has 18 headless tests covering init/master-chain construction, cue scheduling, per-cue rate limiting, stereo pan, volume/mute, sfx gating, meter math, and the no-bus seams (timeWarp cents, musicIntensity clamp, musicChord).
- Test Command: npm test (scripts/run-tests.mjs imports every tests/*.test.js into one node process; the new file is picked up automatically, no runner change needed).
- Fixture: tests/helpers/webaudio.js - installAudioStub() installs a stub AudioContext as global.window.AudioContext and returns { instances, restore() }. Param objects record the last setTargetAtTime value in __lastTarget (deterministic volume/mute assertions); nodes record __connections + start/stop counts; the analyser returns a flat 0.25 frame (peak = rms = 0.25 exact). Test-only: the runner only loads tests/*.test.js and the build only bundles src/+assets/, so it is never shipped or executed.
- Harness: tests/audio.test.js - fresh(t) gives every test a new stub + new createAudio() and restores the previous global window in t.after (all test files share one process). 18 tests, node:assert/strict.
- Deliberate Boundary: the tests NEVER call music(true)/ambience(true) - those schedule recurring setTimeouts (chord tick 18s, pluck ~1s) plus 1.2-1.3s disconnect tails that keep the node event loop alive (the in-process runner would hang). The bus seams are exercised in their no-bus state, which is the same state main.js starts them in.
- Repeatability: fully deterministic - the stub clock starts at 0 and tests advance ctx.currentTime manually across the per-cue min windows; no real audio, no timers, no network; standalone run ~80ms.
- Verification: parse node --check clean (both new files via .mjs copy); standalone node tests/audio.test.js 18/18; npm test 565/565 (+18); build 0. Light browser smoke ?nocache=180a (real Enter -> PLAYING): audio.state() 'running' (real WebAudio via the gesture), meter() live {peak 0.163, rms 0.071}, musicOn true, played {wave 1, swish 1, hit 1} (real cues fired in live play), ship pos finite, console 0 errors / 0 warnings. Zero src/ changes.
- Changed Files: tests/helpers/webaudio.js (new), tests/audio.test.js (new), CHANGELOG.md, production/iterations.md, production/session-state/active.md.
- Result: DONE. ST-180 closed.
- Risk/follow-up: the stub is only as complete as the WebAudio surface audio.js uses today - a future audio.js change that adds a new WebAudio API call will throw a clear missing-method error in the suite (the intended tripwire). Music/ambience bus internals (chord glides, pluck scheduling, sidechain duck) remain untested headless; if E9 (ST-207+) or a music rework touches them, extend the stub with a fake-timer variant. Next I181 ST-181 browser verify E8E (audio on/off, reduced motion).

## I181 - E8E browser verify: audio on/off + reduced motion (ST-181)
- Story: ST-181 | Goal: browser-verify the E8E arc end to end - audio on/off and reduced motion against a live build (acceptance: verification pass + evidence). Verification-only iteration: zero code/test changes.
- Summary: two fresh-tab sessions on dev :5173 exercised the full audio stack and the reduced-motion behavior through real keypresses (Playwright browser_press_key - the WebAudio autoplay policy requires a real user gesture for audio to start), with all state read through window.__vr.
- Launch: dev server up; fresh tabs ?nocache=181a / ?nocache=181b both boot to MENU, __vr live, console 0 errors / 0 warnings (checked at level warning, which includes errors).
- Basic Play: real Enter -> PLAYING (state read via __vr.world.game.state, a property on the GameState machine); both sessions reach a live run; Part 1's idle run dies naturally -> GAME_OVER -> Enter restart -> PLAYING (the full state cycle through the real input path, no forced death needed).
- Audio on/off (Part 1): pre-gesture audio.state() 'uninitialized' with no buses (the I158 gesture gate holds); post-Enter 'running' + musicOn/ambOn true + live meter peak 0.162 (the bed is audible) + chord Am index 0 + played {wave 1}; N mid-run -> musicOn false + 'MUSIC OFF' toast + localStorage voidrunner.settings music:false (I171 persisted toggle) -> N again -> 'MUSIC ON' + music:true (round-trips mid-run); idle death -> GAME_OVER stops the bus (musicOn false, the I158 PLAYING gate) -> Enter restart -> PLAYING, bus back, meter 0.191; M -> meter peak/rms exactly 0 with musicOn still true (the muted master gain: the bed runs under it) -> M again -> recovers 0.185; setVolume(0.2) -> meter 0.060 (proportional to 0.185 at 0.7) -> restored 0.7.
- Reduced motion (Part 2, seeded reducedMotion:true + music:true, reload, real Enter): baseline PLAYING, audio 'running', musicOn true, shakeMag 0, dangerOp 0, warpCents 0, meter peak 0.17; injected hit (world.emit('hit', {pos, knock, lives:3}) + advance(0.05) in one synchronous evaluate - the ship dies when idle, so emit+advance must be atomic) -> eventTally.hit +1, audio played.hit +1 (SFX motion-independent), shakeMag 0 (the I175 shake stays motion-gated), dangerOp 0.6 (the ST-176 reduced-motion damage vignette armed - the motion-safe visual half of the hit), warpCents -514.0625 (the I174 death slow-mo armed under reduced motion - the audio half of the hit), meter 0.139, musicOn true; advance(1.0) -> warpCents exactly 0 (the 0.8s decay completes), dangerOp 0 (the vignette faded), meter 0.186, ship pos finite. Combined with I174's motion-ON smoke (-514.0625 -> 0 exact), the death slow-mo is verified under both motion states.
- Smoke Result: PASS. Both sessions console 0 errors / 0 warnings; ship pos finite scans 0 non-finite. Every G8.4 criterion that has a browser-verifiable half is now evidenced: music audible + toggleable + gesture-gated, death slow-mo (both motion states), all new FX respect reduced motion. The perf + memory-within-budget half is staged to I184 (perf profile) / I185 (memory audit) per the phase-gate precedent (G8.0 recorded a perf reference and left per-phase re-baselining to the phase gates).
- Verification evidence: .evidence/I181/I181_1_reduced_motion_audio.png (reduced-motion PLAYING, red edge vignette visible); the numeric transcript above (exact values, both parts); localStorage read after each N press (music false -> true); settings reset to defaults after the run.
- Changed Files: docs only - CHANGELOG.md, production/iterations.md, production/session-state/active.md.
- Result: DONE. ST-181 closed.
- Risk/follow-up: the audio bus internals (chord glides, pluck scheduling, sidechain duck) are verified as alive + audible (meter peaks, chord index, played counters), not sample-accurate; the I180 headless suite deliberately does not open the buses (recurring timers + 1.2-1.3s disconnect tails would hang the in-process runner) - a fake-timer stub extension remains the follow-up if a music rework lands (E9, ST-207+). Reduced-motion audio was verified on this machine's WebAudio only; the node graph uses standard WebAudio nodes, so no cross-browser audio divergence is expected, but that is a labeled assumption, not a measured fact. Next I182 ST-182 G8.4 gate (cgs-gate-check).

## I182 - G8.4 E8E gate (cgs-gate-check) (ST-182)
- Story: ST-182 | Goal: advisory phase gate for the E8E arc (acceptance: gate PASS, doc) - cgs-gate-check contract (criteria, evidence, concerns, verdict, next decision).
- Summary: phase gate closing the E8E feel/audio/juice arc (I170-I181) before the tail (I183 feel tuning 2, I184 perf, I185 memory, I186 docs). Full criteria/evidence/concerns in production/gates.md (I182 section).
- Gate Criteria (epics.md G8.4): (1) music audible + toggleable + gesture-gated; (2) death slow-mo verified; (3) perf + memory within budget; (4) all new FX respect reduced motion. Plus the standing baseline: fresh npm test + build, console 0/0.
- Evidence: baseline fresh npm test 565/565 + build exit 0 (this iteration). (1) I181 same-day evidence: pre-gesture 'uninitialized' no buses -> post-Enter 'running' meter peaks 0.162-0.191 (audible); N persisted toggle round-trip + M session mute (meter exactly 0, bed under muted master, recovers 0.185) + setVolume proportional (0.060 at 0.2); GAME_OVER stops the bus / restart resumes (I158 gate). (3) pickup/overdrive flash 0.15 - visibility rides the ST-177 per-type sparkle (24 particles at FX high) + SFX + magnet trail; if pickups ever read as invisible, raise the sparkle COUNT, not the flash.

## I184 - E8E deep perf profile (cgs-perf-profile) (ST-184)
- Story: ST-184 | Goal: perf profile (new entities) + optimize (acceptance: perf notes, browser) - cgs-perf-profile contract (Summary, Metric, Budget, Bottleneck, Optimization, Blocking issues, Changed files, Verification evidence, Next owner).
- Summary: docs-only re-baseline of the E8E build (post I170-I183) against the I95 profile, same I128 method (fixed-step advance(1/60) + per-frame renderer.info, manual reset, autoReset off) measured on BOTH render paths - the direct renderer.render path (the I128/I182 gate method) and the real bloom composer path the loop calls at high FX (the I95 method). Steady state is in budget at every realistic load; the one standing finding is unchanged from I95 (the one-time cold first-gameplay-frame lazy-WebGL-init hitch); memory is flat over a 6s worst-case soak (no leak). Zero source changes.
- Metric: full table in production/perf-profile.md "## I184" section. Headline: MENU idle 22 calls / 268 tris (bloom mean 0.58 ms); organic early (~10 rocks) bloom p95 2.2 ms / direct p95 1.3 ms; forced worst case (24 M rocks + a LIVE wave-5 Monolith hp-protected so it survives the soak + up to 14 enemy bullets + real mouse-hold sustained fire, 3 windows x 120 frames) GPU PEAK 49 calls / 2542 tris, bloom p95 2.9-3.5 ms (one GC/compositor window 4.6 / 11.6 / 19.4). First-frame hitch 129-586 ms over 3 fresh cold loads (184c 575.7 gap + 10.2 render, 184d 121 + 39.4, 184e 77 + 52.2), one-time (next frames 1-4 ms), within I95's 90-600 ms range.
- Memory: worst-case 3x120-frame soak (360 frames ~ 6 s sim) heap 15890 -> 15826 KB = -64 KB (flat, no per-frame leak); 19 geoms / 16 tex at peak (bounded). The deep entity-pooling audit is ST-185 / I185.
- Budget: 60 fps = 16.67 ms/frame (aim <14 ms). Frame time PASS (p95 1.3-3.5 ms in both steady scenes); draw calls PASS (peak 49, far under the ~100 ceiling, per-object shared geometry/material); triangles PASS (peak 2542, trivial for WebGL); GPU memory PASS (19 geoms / 16 tex, bounded).
- Bottleneck: no steady-state CPU or GPU problem. The single cost is the one-time cold first-gameplay-frame lazy WebGL init (shader compile + GPU buffer/texture upload) plus transition-tick main-thread work (WebAudio graph + 0.5 s noise-buffer build, wave-1 setup). It splits variably between a pre-render main-thread gap (77-576 ms) and an elevated first render call (10-52 ms); user-perceived cost is the sum (~129-586 ms), one-time per session.
- Optimization: the I95 offscreen warm-up render remains the proposed fix (populate one of each pooled entity + one-shot effect, render once to a 2x2 RT, tear down); a bare renderer.compile() was already proven insufficient in I95 (shader-only, no buffer/texture upload; reverted). Restated as the standing decision - implement in the final wrap if budget allows, else ship with the documented one-time hitch. No code changed this iteration.
- Changed Files: production/perf-profile.md (new "## I184" section). No src/ or tests/ changes (docs-only, like the I95 profile and the I182 gate).
- Result: DONE. ST-184 closed. 575/575 (unchanged); build 0; browser smokes (184c/f + worst-case scene) console 0/0, ship finite, NaN 0, boss finite.
- Risk/follow-up: frame-time numbers use the I128 fixed-step advance+render bracket (not the raw rAF callback), so they are not 1:1 comparable to I95's rAF-loop means - reported per-method. Heap samples are performance.memory (Chromium, approximate, no forced GC). The first-frame hitch is a startup warning, not a release blocker. Next I185 ST-185 memory audit (new entity pooling).

NO LEAKS FOUND: all core stores are bounded capped arrays with in-place compaction (rocks 24 + belt layer 5, bullets 64, enemy bolts 24, mines 10, drones 6, drops 1); every transient (mine pendingFires, drone decayed, Splicer _pending, vortex pending, the ST-074 event double buffer) is drained or reset per tick; render pools are sized from the same CONFIG keys (asteroidRenderer 24+5, bulletPool 64, enemyBulletMesh 24, mineMesh 10, droneMesh 6, powerupMesh 4, one 512-slot particle ring, 12 pre-created popup divs, kill feed <= 5 rows). GPU live resources step up ONLY when each content class first renders, then plateau flat (geoms 14 -> 30 by t=390 s, tex 15 -> 22, zero steps in the final 210 s - no leak signature). JS heap is a bounded GC sawtooth (12.9-25.3 MB) with a stable floor (per-100 s minima 12854/14940/15967/15119/14748/15777 KB - no drift; peaks are content- + SFX-churn-driven). DOM constant (29 body children = MENU baseline, kill-feed rows <= 5, popups fixed 12). Zero cap/transient/DOM violations across all samples. The one-time lazy GPU upload steps (14 -> 30 geoms over the first ~390 s) are the same artifact class as the I184 first-frame hitch - the I95 offscreen warm-up render remains the standing proposed fix (owner decision, deferred, unchanged). ## I187 - Context-loss hardening (re-init path) (ST-187)

- Skill: cgs-bugfix (proactive-hardening variant - no user-visible defect; the I75 baseline left the re-init path unprobeable and the loop kept doing sim/HUD work while the GL context was dead).
- Summary: named the webglcontextlost/restored handlers so the re-init path is probeable, added a ctxLost loop guard so the rAF loop does ZERO sim/render work while the context is dead, added re-init on restore (bloom composer render targets re-created, renderer.info reset, and a NEW clock.reset() so the stall interval never becomes a dt spike on the first restored frame), and a framesRendered loop-work counter. Smoke also caught and fixed an I187 line-merge that swallowed the I75 __vr.ctxOverlay probe into a comment (exposed probe silently gone - restored on its own line).
- Reproduction (the gap I75 left): I75 handlers were inline arrows - not probeable; the loop kept calling tick/sync/renderer.info/adaptive-quality every frame while the context was dead (three.js render() is a no-op while lost, but the sim kept advancing toward a dead context and the HUD kept updating); no clock re-init on restore (the first restored frame could eat the whole stall as dt - only the ST-078 0.25s clamp + 8-substep backlog backstopped it); a real GL loss cannot be forced in headless Chromium, so evidence goes through handler-path probes (simCtxLost/simCtxRestore call the real named handlers with a synthetic preventDefault).
- Minimal Fix (src/main.js only):
  1. let ctxLost = false + let framesRendered = 0 (loop-work counter).
  2. onCtxLost(e) (named): preventDefault (mark restorable), ctxLost = true, world.pause() if PLAYING, overlay flex + I75 initial status text, 10s escalation timer (I75 behavior preserved).
  3. onCtxRestored() (named): clearTimeout, renderer.setSize, bloom.resize (composer RTs died with the GL context - resize re-creates them; three.js re-uploads geoms/texs/programs lazily), renderer.info.reset(), clock.reset(performance.now()) (I187 re-init: stall time discarded, no dt spike on the first restored frame; ST-078 clamp is the backstop), ctxLost = false, overlay hidden.
  4. Loop guard at the top of frame(): if (ctxLost) return; - skips tick/sync/info/adaptive while dead.
  5. framesRendered++ after bloom.render().
  6. Probes: __vr.ctxLost(), __vr.simCtxLost(), __vr.simCtxRestore(), __vr.framesRendered(); restored the I75 __vr.ctxOverlay exposure (smoke-caught regression).
- Regression Test: I188 (ST-188) is the dedicated evidence story (repeated cycles in both states, escalation timing, NaN/finite scans, screenshots); the probes are the seam.
- Evidence:
  - 581/581 green; node scripts/build.mjs exit 0; node --check (ESM) clean.
  - Browser smoke (Playwright, ?nocache=187b/187c, REAL Enter gesture; rAF in this headless Chromium throttles variably ~1-56 ticks/s):
    - C (PLAYING baseline): 117 frames / 2.7s window (187b); 3/3 (187c, throttled tick) - loop renders when not lost.
    - D (simCtxLost from PLAYING): ctxLost=true, state PAUSED, overlay flex, status = initial "The browser dropped the 3D rendering context..." text; screenshot .evidence/I187/I187_1_ctx_loss_overlay.png.
    - E (guard - the key assertion): 152 rAF ticks fired (187b) / 3 ticks (187c) yet framesDelta = 0 in both; world.time bit-identical across the 2.7s window (1.0666666666666678 -> same); rock pos bit-identical (187b: 3.7117490637942008 -> same).
    - F (simCtxRestore): ctxLost=false, overlay none, state stays PAUSED (restore does NOT auto-resume - the pause screen takes over).
    - G (recovery): post-restore window framesDelta 2-3 > 0 (loop alive); world.resume() -> PLAYING; advance(0.5) -> timeDelta 0.4999999999999982 (fixed substeps), ship + all rocks finite.
    - H (timer discipline): 5 rapid lost/restore cycles -> final lost: ctxLost=true + overlay flex (no double-fire, no leaked timer); final restore clean.
    - I (MENU path): Q from PAUSED -> MENU; simCtxLost leaves state MENU (no pause), overlay flex; window framesDelta 0; restore -> loop resumes in MENU (framesDelta 3).
  - Console 0 errors / 0 warnings across all smoke pages.
  - Bonus real-world signal: on the earlier 187a page a REAL headless GL loss/restore event pair fired mid-smoke (GPU-process churn); afterwards the page showed overlay=none, ctxLost=false, loop running - the handlers survived a genuine cycle unaided.
- Risks:
  - First post-restore frame = one-time lazy WebGL re-upload (geoms/texs/programs), same one-time hitch class as I95's cold first-gameplay frame; documented, not fixed here (post-restore re-upload is inherently lazy in three.js r160).
  - sim probes exercise the real handler path but not the renderer's own lost/restore internals; I188 adds evidence depth (repeated cycles, escalation text timing, NaN scans).
  - clock.reset(performance.now()) discards the stall interval - correct for a game (no fast-forward after context death), consistent with ST-078 resume semantics.
  - Smoke methodology note: an initial G-step NaN was caused by MY probe input (thrust: 0, a number) - advance()'s documented intent is {thrust:{x,y}, aimPoint, firing}; a number gives thrust.x = undefined -> NaN in Ship.step. Game code is correct; re-ran with the correct vector (clean). No validation added to the debug probe (minimalism).
- Changed files: src/main.js ONLY.
- Verification: npm test 581/581; build exit 0; ESM parse clean; smoke A-I as above; console 0/0.
- Result: DONE. ST-187 closed - E8F (ST-187..206) open at I187. Next I188 ST-188 context-loss test + verify (browser).
## I188 - Context-loss test + verify (evidence) (ST-188)

- Skill: cgs-test-evidence-review (Summary / Evidence / Gap / Confidence / Follow-up / Risks / Changed files / Verification evidence / Next owner).
- Summary: dedicated evidence pass for the I187 context-loss hardening. Exercised the named handler paths (simCtxLost/simCtxRestore = the real onCtxLost/onCtxRestored) across repeated loss/restore cycles in BOTH game states, the 10s escalation timer, the I187 clock re-init, and world-graph finiteness under load, plus a 60s organic loop-health soak. One minimal test-seam addition: the __vr.clockState() probe (ST-078 clock internals) so the re-init mechanism - not just its effects - is directly observable.
- Evidence (Playwright, fresh page ?nocache=188a, REAL Enter gestures; rAF in this headless Chromium is bursty/throttled ~1-56 fps varying, so time-correlation claims go through the deterministic advance() seam where noted):
  - T0 baseline: fresh run advanced 2s (5 rocks, wave 1); deep finiteness scan of the whole world graph (depth 4) = exactly ONE non-finite, the legit scoreKeeper.lastKillTime = -Infinity sentinel ("no kill yet"). clockState probe live: {step 1/60, maxFrameDt 0.25, maxSubsteps 8, acc 0}.
  - T1 PLAYING hammer (10 cycles: lost -> 600ms freeze -> restore): every per-phase assertion exact - lost: ctxLost=true + PAUSED + overlay flex; freeze: framesDelta 0 AND world.timeDelta 0; restore: ctxLost=false + PAUSED + overlay none. All 10/10. (world.time advanced +0.6667s in the PLAYING gap BETWEEN tool calls before the first loss - the real loop running normally; T1 itself was fully frozen.)
  - T2 MENU hammer (5 cycles): state stays MENU throughout (onCtxLost only pauses from PLAYING - by design), guard holds (framesDelta 0, timeDelta 0), restores clean. 5/5. Screenshot .evidence/I188/I188_1_menu_ctx_lost.png (overlay over the menu).
  - T3 escalation timer: lost -> initial status text; after 11s wall the status is EXACTLY "Still no signal after 10s. If it does not come back, reload the game." with ctxLost=true / PAUSED / overlay flex still in effect; restore clean; re-lost -> status back to the INITIAL text (clearTimeout works, timer re-arms); at +3s still initial (no premature escalation).
  - T4 clock re-init (the I187 mechanism, direct): before loss clock.last=107213.1; during a 1210ms stall clock.last UNCHANGED (the loop guard skips clock.tick - the clock is not merely bypassed, it is untouched); after restore clock.last=109204.9 with |last - performance.now()| = 0ms and acc = 0. The 1210ms stall was discarded - pre-I187 code would have injected min(1.21, 0.25) = 0.25s (15 substeps) of sim into the first restored frame.
  - T5 no backlog: resume -> advance(1.0) -> world.time delta 0.9999999999999969 (exactly 60 fixed substeps, no accumulated stall, no catch-up burst).
  - T6 finiteness under load (5 cycles interleaved with live combat - resume + advance(0.5) each cycle with thrust and alternating fire): all 5 advances ran deterministically (timeDelta exactly 0.5 each), the fight was real (rocks 5 -> 9 with splits, up to 3 live bullets, score 127 from kills); post-battery world-graph scan: ZERO non-finite anywhere (the T0 sentinel legitimately resolved to a finite timestamp by the first kill). (Method note: the first T6 variant forgot to resume after restore, so its advances were no-ops under the World.step PLAYING gate - the gate is correct behavior; T6b fixed the harness, not the game.)
  - T8 organic 60s idle soak after the whole battery (new run via real Enter, no input): 6 samples at 10s - framesRendered strictly monotonic (10/20/30/40/50/60), sim time advanced with the ST-078 clamp in effect (1.33s sim per 10s wall - bursty headless rAF, no backlog), ship DRIFTED INTO a rock and died organically (lives 3 -> 2, respawn handled, score 15), every sample finite, final state PLAYING, ctxLost=false.
  - Console: 0 errors / 0 warnings across the full page lifetime (3 total messages, all info-level three.js boot logs).
  - Prior-session bonus signal (I187): a REAL headless GL loss/restore event pair fired organically on the 187a page; the handlers survived unaided (overlay back to none, loop running).
- Gap:
  1. sim probes exercise the real handler path but NOT the renderer's own lost/restore internals (WebGL buffer/program re-upload). One organic real event was observed (I187) but the re-upload cost was not measured in headless.
  2. First post-restore frame hitch (lazy re-upload) is documented as a mechanism, unquantified here (GPU-dependent; out of reach in headless).
  3. Escalation boundary precision (9.9s vs 10.1s) not pinned - single 11s wait + code inspection (10000ms setTimeout); the no-premature-escalation half IS asserted.
  4. Wall-time <-> sim-time correlation is not assertable in this headless environment (bursty rAF); the deterministic advance() seam carries the sim-side assertions.
- Confidence: HIGH for handler-path behavior (directly probed, repeated 25+ times across both states, deterministic assertions). MEDIUM for real-GL-loss equivalence (handler survival observed once organically; re-upload internals unmeasured).
- Follow-up: real-GPU first-restore-frame measurement = I202 human/playtest territory (a normal desktop browser with real WebGL churn). No code follow-up needed from this evidence.
- Risks: none new introduced (one-line probe). The -Infinity sentinel is a pre-existing legit pattern (combo-window timer), not a defect.

## I189 - Visibility-pause hardening (auto-pause, dt clamp) (ST-189)

- Skill: cgs-bugfix (proactive-hardening variant: Summary / Reproduction / Minimal Fix / Regression Test / Evidence / Risks / Changed files / Verification evidence / Next owner).
- Summary: the I43 inline auto-pause arrow (unprobeable, decision logic in the DOM layer, untestable) is now a named handler (main.js) that delegates to a new pure core module, src/core/visibilityPause.js (visibilityAction: 'pause' only when visibility==='hidden' AND state==='PLAYING', else 'none'). The policy is documented deliberately in the module header: (a) pause on hidden-while-PLAYING because the tab's rAF stalls anyway - the player gets the pause screen instead of a surprise death, and the existing ST-078 clock (maxFrameDt 0.25 + clock.reset on PAUSED->PLAYING) discards the stall so no dt spike on resume; (b) NO auto-resume on 'visible' - a surprise resume across a tab switch can kill the ship while the player looks away; same "restore does not auto-resume" policy as the I187 ctx-restore path. The dt-clamp path was evidenced, not re-built.
- Reproduction: the gap was the I43 line document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', () => {...world.pause()}) - an inline arrow cannot be driven from outside and the decision lived in the DOM layer. A real tab-hide cannot be forced in headless Chromium, so the browser evidence drives the REAL named handler via the new __vr.simVisibility(v) probe: it shadows document.visibilityState with a temporary own property, calls onVisibilityChange(), then deletes the shadow (own-property delete restores the prototype getter - verified).
- Minimal Fix: (1) NEW src/core/visibilityPause.js - VISIBILITY constants + visibilityAction(visibility, state), pure, DOM-free, ~1.6KB, header documents both policy decisions. (2) src/main.js - imports visibilityAction; inline arrow replaced by named function onVisibilityChange() (two lines); new __vr.simVisibility probe. (3) NEW tests/visibilityPause.test.js.
- Regression Test: tests/visibilityPause.test.js, 10 tests - exhaustive 4x2 visibility x state matrix (exactly one pause cell), hidden x {MENU, PAUSED, GAME_OVER} -> none, visible x all states -> none, unknown visibility strings (prerender/unloaded/''/undefined/null) -> none, unknown/case-mismatched states -> none, exact-match semantics (not substring, not case-insensitive), contract values only ('pause' | 'none').
- Evidence (Playwright, page ?nocache=189a; transition spam asserted via world.game.transitions length deltas):
  - V1 PLAYING -> simVisibility('hidden') -> PAUSED, exactly 1 transition recorded.
  - V2 hidden again while PAUSED -> stays PAUSED, 0 new transitions (no double-pause spam).
  - V3 simVisibility('visible') while PAUSED -> stays PAUSED (no auto-resume), 0 transitions.
  - V4 resume -> PLAYING; simVisibility('prerender') -> stays PLAYING, 0 transitions (defensive unknown value).
  - V5b PLAYING -> hidden -> 1.2 s real sleep: state PAUSED, world.time bit-frozen (delta 0); visible -> still PAUSED; resume -> after 2 rAFs, clockState().last within 100 ms of performance.now and acc=0 (ST-078 reset verified directly); then advance(1.0) -> exactly 1.0 (no backlog burst).
  - V6realEvent document.dispatchEvent(new Event('visibilitychange')) with the real visibilityState='visible' -> no state change, 0 transitions, getter intact.
  - V7 MENU path: P -> PAUSED, Q -> MENU, then simVisibility('hidden') -> stays MENU (no pause), simVisibility('visible') -> MENU, 0 new transitions total.
  - npm test 591/591 (581 + 10 new); node scripts/build.mjs exit 0; node --check (ESM) clean; console 0/0.
- Method note: the first V5 attempt read clockState() synchronously right after world.resume() - the ST-078 clock reset happens in the NEXT rAF frame (inside frame()), not synchronously in resume(), so the assertion failed spuriously. Re-ran after 2 rAFs (V5b). Probe-timing artifact, not a game bug.
- Risks: __vr.simVisibility is not a real tab-hide - in a genuinely hidden tab rAF stops for real; the ST-078 clamp covers the rAF-stall case even if the visibilitychange event is delayed or missing, so dt-spike protection does not depend on this handler firing. No-auto-resume is a deliberate UX policy - confirm at the I202 human playtest.
- Changed files: src/main.js (named handler + simVisibility probe ONLY), NEW src/core/visibilityPause.js, NEW tests/visibilityPause.test.js.
- Verification evidence: V1-V7 above; 591/591; build 0; ESM parse clean; console 0/0.
- Result: DONE. ST-189 closed. Next I190 ST-190 replay viewer + capture UI (browser) [CUTTABLE - assess vs scope-check-E8.md cap].

## I190 - Replay viewer (watch DEMO PILOT + saved replays) (cgs-dev-story) (ST-190)

- Skill: cgs-dev-story (Summary / Story Goal / Owner / Dependencies / Acceptance Tests / Risks / Changed files / Verification evidence / Next owner or decision).
- Story Goal: let the player watch how a run went - the built-in DEMO PILOT plus their own saved runs (ST-191) - replayed through the SAME deterministic sim the live game runs, so a watched run is ground truth, not a re-approximation.
- Summary: new pure core codec src/core/replay.js (quantized 1/25 RLE intent tape + strict decoder that rejects tampered payloads + the built-in DEMO PILOT, a canonical deterministic script: seed 1909, 2220 substeps, pilot dies at step 2169 with a small tail) and a new REPLAY state: MENU -> REPLAY -> (PAUSED <-> REPLAY) / REPLAY -> GAME_OVER / GAME_OVER -> MENU. World gains replayMode + replayBegin() + a simActive getter (step() advances in PLAYING or REPLAY); resume() returns to REPLAY when replayMode. main.js: startReplayView/exitReplayView/restartReplay + replayIntent() feeding the recorded intents into the fixed-step tick (live input bypassed while REPLAY); speeds x1/x2/x4 on keys 1/2/3; P pause, R restart the tape from step 0, Q quit (also from a finished replay), Enter on a finished replay quits to the menu; a watched run NEVER touches the scoreboards (the gameover event handler is gated on !replayView - no setBest / recordScore / recordDaily / NEW BEST). Menu REPLAYS button + screen-replays panel (DEMO PILOT row + saved runs newest-first, cap CONFIG.storage.replaysMax=2; '- no saved replays yet -' when empty; BACK). Slim replay HUD bar (src/render/replayHud.js: title, score/wave line, xN speed, key hints). storage.js: getReplays()/saveReplay() with strict decode validation (defensive against corrupt localStorage blobs). Probes: __vr.replayView()/startReplay()/exitReplay()/demoReplay()/replayHud.
- Determinism fix found in smoke: two consecutive demo runs on ONE page world were bit-identical except entity IDs - nextId counters are page-lifetime and clear() never reset them. Fix: clear() in the six core entity stores (asteroids/mines/drones/powerups/weapons/enemyBullets) now resets nextId = 1. Safe by construction: clear() is called only from World.reset() (grep-verified) and the render pools map by array index, never by entity ID, so a fresh ID space per run is invisible to the render layer. Regression test added: 'two consecutive demo runs on ONE world are bit-identical (IDs included)'.
- Owner: /root (single-agent run).
- Dependencies: I189 committed (975bcbc); deterministic core (all randomness through src/core/rng.js); the storage module lineage (I66 top-5 / I141 daily); CONFIG.replay + CONFIG.storage.replays* keys added this iteration.
- Acceptance Tests: tests/replay.test.js (19 tests): quantize/dequantize round-trip within 1/q; null aim disambiguated from aim at (0,0); quantization clamps to +/-40q; defensive quantize against garbage fields; RLE constant intent -> one entry; alternation -> one entry per step with runs summing to len; maxSteps truncation (flag + capped length, runs still sum to len); strict decode accepts a valid tape + the demo payload, rejects a corrupt battery, enforces maxLen for demos too, and clamps tampered meta (NaN/negative/huge -> sane ints); createPlayer returns quantized intents within 1/q (neutral past the end); the demo pilot drives canonical thrusts exactly and is neutral past len; recorded-tape playback is bit-identical across two fresh worlds; demo pilot (seed 1909) playback is deterministic and reaches GAME_OVER; two consecutive demo runs on ONE world are bit-identical (IDs included); storage: getReplays starts empty / saveReplay keeps newest-first with the cap / saveReplay rejects invalid payloads ({saved:false}, nothing written) / getReplays drops corrupt stored entries. Plus tests/state.test.js (REPLAY transition table) and tests/visibilityPause.test.js (a hidden REPLAY also auto-pauses).
- Risks: the viewer is a new surface for tampered localStorage data - mitigated by the strict decoder (bad payloads rejected; getReplays drops bad entries; quantized coords clamped to +/-40q so a tape can never carry a runaway coordinate into the sim). The fresh-ID-space fix changes entity IDs across World.reset() - safe because nothing persists or compares IDs across resets (index-based render pools; the I190 regression test pins the bit-identity). A finished replay shows GAME_OVER with 'PRESS Q TO QUIT TO MENU' and never writes a NEW BEST (smoke R3: scores JSON byte-identical before/after).
- Changed files: NEW src/core/replay.js, NEW src/render/replayHud.js, NEW tests/replay.test.js; src/core/state.js (REPLAY state + transition table), src/core/game.js (replayMode/replayBegin/simActive/resume/restart-clear), src/core/config.js (replay block + storage replays keys), src/core/storage.js (getReplays/saveReplay), src/core/visibilityPause.js (REPLAY pauses on hidden), src/main.js (viewer wiring + key handling + probes + scoreboard gate), src/render/screens.js (REPLAYS button + replays panel + replay-mode game-over prompt + REPLAY panel slot), tests/state.test.js, tests/visibilityPause.test.js, and the six clear() ID resets: src/core/asteroids.js, src/core/mines.js, src/core/drones.js, src/core/powerups.js, src/core/weapons.js, src/core/enemyBullets.js.
- Verification evidence: 615/615 tests (614 + 1 new regression); node scripts/build.mjs exit 0; node --check clean on every edited file. Browser smoke (?nocache=190b/190c/190r5; screenshots .evidence/I190/): R1 menu REPLAYS panel (DEMO PILOT row + '- no saved replays yet -' + BACK; menu hidden behind the panel, state MENU) -> I190_1_replays_panel.png; R2 watch the demo (replayView {idx, length 2220, speed 1, mode demo}, all full-screen panels hidden, simActive + replayMode true, HUD title/bar/x1/hints visible) -> I190_2_replay_hud.png; keys P -> PAUSED (HUD gone, world.time bit-frozen) / P -> REPLAY resume / 2 -> x2 / 3 -> x4 / P,Q -> MENU (replayView null, time 0); R3 a full deterministic demo run to GAME_OVER (t=36.15, score 11334, kills 57, wave 2, maxCombo 15, 57/198 shots) with NO NEW BEST and the voidrunner.scores JSON byte-identical before/after, 'PRESS Q TO QUIT TO MENU' shown, Q -> MENU -> I190_3_replay_gameover.png; R4 two consecutive demos on ONE page world deep-equal (IDs included - rock ids 49,54,55,56,64,66..74 in both runs) after the fresh-ID-space fix (pre-fix the same pair differed only in IDs; the regression test now pins this); R5 finiteness (depth-7 recursive scan of __vr.world): replay graph after advance(20) 460 numbers / 0 non-finite; live PLAYING graph 437 numbers / exactly 1 non-finite = the intentional sentinel scoreKeeper.lastKillTime = -Infinity (src/core/score.js:13 'no kill yet' marker; t - (-Infinity) > window resets the combo - deterministic by construction); console 0 errors / 0 warnings (only expected adaptive-quality INFO lines) -> I190_4_live_finiteness_scan.png.
- Result: DONE. ST-190 closed. Next I191 ST-191 replay capture UI (O-key record toggle + save-on-game-over).
## I191 - Replay capture UI (O-key record + save-on-game-over) (cgs-dev-story) (ST-191)

- Skill: cgs-dev-story (Summary / Story Goal / Owner / Dependencies / Acceptance Tests / Risks / Changed files / Verification evidence / Next owner or decision).
- Summary: ST-191 - press O during a live run to record it; on game over the tape is saved to REPLAYS storage; a WATCH THIS RUN button appears on the game-over screen for just-recorded runs; every run now gets a fresh random seed so a recorded run is fully reproducible.
- Story Goal: let the player capture their own run and watch it back in the I190 viewer - recording is one key, saving is automatic, watching is one click.
- Owner: gameplay-programmer (root agent, single session).
- Dependencies: I190 (REPLAY state machine, src/core/replay.js codec + createRecorder, storage.getReplays/saveReplay cap 2, REPLAYS panel, replay HUD).
- Acceptance Tests: 5 new in tests/recording.test.js - (1) O starts only in PLAYING (MENU/PAUSED/GAME_OVER/REPLAY no-op); (2) O stops an active recording, a stopped one cannot be re-armed; (3) exact-match state semantics (unknown/case-mismatched states no-op); (4) save only a non-empty recording of a non-watched run (started-but-empty not saved; a watched replay never saves); (5) ST-191 flow: record a live pilot run (seed 777, 3600 substeps), decode, replay twice - two replays of the same tape are bit-identical and the replay matches the live original on aliveness, wave, kills, score, end time (<= 6 substeps) and ship position (< 1 unit).
- Quantization evidence (why CONFIG.replay.quant 25 -> 1000): node harness (seed 777, 3600-step pilot) measured original vs replay of its own tape: q=25 the replay diverges catastrophically (original survives 60s / score 21420 / wave 4; replay dies t=32.9 / score 10952 / wave 2 - 1/25 aim error cascades in a chaotic sim); q=100 still diverges (scoreDiff -5114, shipDist 8.6); q=1000 outcome-identical (same aliveness/wave/kills/score, timeDiff 0.000, shipDist 0.0007). q=1000 is the decoder max (valid 4..1000). Tape cost at q=1000: ~98 KB for a 60s worst case (varying aim every substep) vs maxTapeBytes 700000. The demo pilot (kind demo) drives pilotIntent live and is unaffected - I190 numbers (seed 1909, dies t=36.15, score 11334, kills 57) stand.
- Behavior change (documented): normal runs now reseed per run (runSeed = freshRunSeed(); world.reseed(runSeed) in startRun/restartRun) - previously they continued the session RNG stream. The tape records the run seed, so a saved run reproduces exactly. Daily mode unchanged (fixed daily seed).
- One-stop O semantics: O starts (PLAYING only), O stops; a stopped recording cannot be re-armed in the same run; a stopped-but-started tape still saves on game over (plays neutral past the stop point).
- Risks: (low) tape size growth with quant - bounded by maxTapeBytes + RLE; (low) per-run reseed changes unrecorded-run score distribution (random, intended; daily unaffected); (accepted) quantization is not lossless, so replay-vs-original asserts outcome fidelity, not bit identity (bit identity is guaranteed between two replays of the same tape).
- Changed files: src/core/recording.js (new - pure toggle/save rules: recordingAction, shouldSaveOnGameOver), src/render/recIndicator.js (new - pulsing REC chip), tests/recording.test.js (new - 5 tests), src/main.js (O key -> toggleRecording; record the exact intent before world.step; per-run reseed; save-on-game-over gated by shouldSaveOnGameOver + !replayView; WATCH THIS RUN wiring; __vr.recording probe + __vr.toggleRecording), src/render/screens.js (WATCH THIS RUN button, shown only when opts.watchRun), src/core/config.js (replay.quant 25 -> 1000 + evidence comment), src/core/replay.js (Q_DEFAULT 1000 + header note).
- Verification evidence: npm test 620/620 (615 + 5 new); npm run build exit 0; browser smoke (?nocache=191a, headless rAF ~2fps, sim driven via __vr.advance, real keys via Playwright) S1-S7: S1 Enter -> PLAYING, O -> REC chip display:flex + toast RECORDING - O TO STOP + active true + steps growing + fresh per-run seed (33835651) -> I191_S1_rec_chip.png; S2 O -> stopped (active false, stopped true, chip hidden, toast RECORDING STOPPED), third O is a no-op (cannot re-arm); S3 recorded run -> GAME_OVER -> toast RUN SAVED TO REPLAYS + replay saved (seed matches runSeed, meta {score 45, wave 4, kills 0, time 151}) + WATCH THIS RUN visible; negative case: unrecorded run -> no save, no button, no NEW BEST; edge: stopped-after-start tape still saves (len 40); cap 2 holds with eviction (3rd save evicted the oldest, panel showed 2 rows) -> I191_S3_gameover_watch.png; S4 WATCH THIS RUN click -> REPLAY (view {idx, length 681, speed 1, meta matching}, HUD bar with progress + x1 + hints visible, no REC chip) -> I191_S4_replay_state.png; tape to end (idx 810/810 on first watch) -> GAME_OVER PRESS Q TO QUIT TO MENU -> Q -> MENU; S5 menu REPLAYS panel lists DEMO PILOT + 2 SAVED RUN rows newest-first (SCORE 30 WAVE 1 0:12 above SCORE 45 WAVE 4 2:31) -> I191_S5_replays_panel.png; S6 O during REPLAY and during replay GAME_OVER is a no-op (recording() stays inactive, chip hidden); S7 finiteness scan (ship/rocks/bullets/score/time) 0 non-finite; console 0 errors / 0 warnings (only expected adaptive-quality INFO lines).
- Result: DONE. ST-191 closed. Next I192 ST-192 test growth (300+ target already met at 620 - I192 = coverage-gap audit + targeted new tests).

## I194 - Security audit - untrusted-input audit of storage/seed codecs (cgs-security-audit) (ST-194)
- Summary: audited every trust boundary that handles untrusted input (localStorage blobs, pasted share codes, saved replays, daily-key date components) plus DOM-sink / secret / network-surface scans. No exploitable defects found; added a deterministic totality test battery (tests/security.test.js) pinning the no-throw / no-prototype-pollution guarantees.
- Secret: none. Scanned all 183 first-party files (git ls-files over src, tests, scripts, docs, design, production, assets + index.html + package.json) with concrete credential patterns (AWS AKIA, sk-, ghp_, xox, Google AIza, JWT eyJ prefix, PRIVATE KEY blocks): 0 hits. (A loose 'token' pattern matched only parser-token code inside vendored three.js libs - not credentials.)
- Attack Surface: (1) localStorage -> Storage getters + migratePayload (tamperable by any same-origin script); (2) pasted share code -> decodeResult (the only free-text user input; current UI only writes share codes to clipboard, never decodes external input, but the codec is now pinned for future use); (3) saved replays -> strict decodeReplay; (4) daily key -> pure fnv1a over explicit date components. Network: zero in src/ (no fetch/XMLHttpRequest/WebSocket/Worker/sendBeacon); the only URL in src/ is the inert w3.org SVG namespace constant (main.js:193, used by createElementNS). Dynamic code: zero eval / new Function in src/. DOM: no innerHTML / insertAdjacentHTML / document.write / outerHTML anywhere in src/; UI uses textContent/createElement; the colorblind-preview SVG is built with createElementNS + fixed attribute strings.
- Exploit Risk: low / none. Every decoder is total: type guards, charset + range validation, FNV checksum (share), strict per-entry RLE validation with run-sum equality (replay), and sanitizeMeta clamping (no NaN/Infinity/unknown-mode can reach the HUD). storage._read wraps JSON.parse in try/catch -> null; migratePayload rejects future/corrupt versions and non-object data. JSON.parse defines __proto__ as an OWN property (DefineProperty semantics), so a crafted blob cannot pollute Object.prototype - now pinned by test.
- Mitigation: storage already whitelists every field; decoders are strict and never throw; the UI never injects HTML. New tests/security.test.js (8 tests, +8 to suite): decodeResult adversarial-string battery (seeded base36 junk 64B-1MB, corrupt checksum, 100k dots, unicode replacement chars, script-injection text, 8 non-string inputs); decodeReplay 27-case malformed battery (bad version/kind, float/negative/2^40 seed, len 0 / 1e8, quant 3/1001/NaN, short/long/string RLE entries, n=0 run, coord clamp overflow, bad aim/fire flags, run-sum mismatch, null-prototype object); __proto__ pollution probe; meta-sanitization pin; 72000-step legitimate-max acceptance; migratePayload corrupt-version/non-object battery; global no-pollution assertion. All junk is generated from a seeded RNG (ST-193 flake rule).
- Blocking issues: none.
- Changed files: tests/security.test.js (new, 8 tests).
- Verification evidence: npm test 630/630 (was 622, +8 new, 0 fail); node scripts/build.mjs exit 0 ("build: dist/ assembled"); no rendering change (browser smoke not required); all scans above reproducible against the git ls-files scope.
- Next owner or decision: I195 ST-195 regression round B (cgs-regression-suite): repeated full-suite runs + spot checks on systems changed since I170.
- Result: DONE. ST-194 closed. Next I195 ST-195 (regression round B).

## I196 - Regression round B part 2 - browser smoke + render-layer regression (cgs-regression-suite) (ST-196)
- Summary: full browser regression pass against the dev server (headless Chromium, Playwright): boot, state machine, recording, replay view, HUD rendering and console health verified through the real page (window.__vr probes + real key events). No render-layer regression; no code changes.
- Critical Path: boot -> MENU (all __vr probes live); Enter -> PLAYING; 8s play (wave 1, lives 3); P -> PAUSED; P -> PLAYING; recording toggle on -> off with a fresh runSeed per run; ~54s sim with no input -> GAME_OVER (lives 0, score 45, wave 2); Enter -> PLAYING (restart). Replay view: MENU -> startReplay(demo payload) -> REPLAY (replayView {idx, length 2220, speed 1, meta sanitized}) -> 27s sim -> GAME_OVER at idx 2169 with score 11334 - EXACT match with the I195 core-probe pinned demo-pilot values (render layer agrees with core); exitReplay -> MENU.

## I197 - Build size + load audit (cgs-perf-profile) (ST-197)
- Summary: audited the download + load side (complement to the I95 frame-time profile): dist/ artifact weight, per-section breakdown, gzip transfer cost, and a live browser load profile. Result: 1,864 KB raw / 449.6 KB gzip first payload - within the adopted budget (<=2 MB raw / <=550 KB gzip) with headroom; 86 resources load fully parallel (no waterfall), all done by 622 ms, domReady 890 ms headless. No blocker; three advisories (host-level compression, irreducible three.js weight, no-bundler request count) - no code changes.
- Metric: dist/ = 1,864 KB raw / 449.6 KB gzip (zlib level 6 per file). Sections: vendor 1,655 KB / 290.3 KB gz (three.module.js 1,243 KB = 65% of dist; GLTFLoader 106 KB; BufferGeometryUtils 31 KB; postprocessing chain 25.6 KB); src ~332 KB / 131.4 KB gz (main 62.6, game 33.5, screens 24.8, config 23.6, audio 20.3 KB); assets 88.1 KB / 27.1 KB gz (5 GLB: L 33.1, ship 22.2, boss 14.4, M 9.2, S 9.2 KB); index+favicon 1.8 KB. Browser load (headless :5173): 86 resources = 81 scripts + 5 GLB fetches, all starting ~77 ms and finishing by 622 ms; domReady 890 ms; loadEvent 924 ms; window.__vr live.
- Budget: adopted for this audit: <= 2 MB raw / <= 550 KB gzip for the whole first payload (desktop-web, broadband target). Measured 1,864 KB / 449.6 KB -> within budget (7% raw / 18% gzip headroom). Assets sub-check: 88.1 KB raw / 27.1 KB gzip for all five Blender models - trivial against the poly budgets already enforced in design/asset-spec.md.
- Bottleneck: (1) three.module.js 1,243 KB raw / ~200 KB of the gzip total - minified official build, irreducible under the no-bundler/no-CDN vendored convention (dominant cost, accepted). (2) 81 module requests - measured fully parallel (no waterfall on HTTP/1.1 localhost; one stream on HTTP/2+); only a pathological HTTP/1.1-only host would make this a real cost. (3) dev/preview servers serve uncompressed (wire measured 1,887 KB raw) - any real static host with gzip/brotli delivers ~450 KB; serve.mjs is a dev convenience, not the shipping path. Unverified assumption: none - all numbers measured live this iteration.
- Optimization: advisories only, no changes: (1) LOW - enable gzip/brotli at the host level for slow-link targets (-76% transfer); (2) LOW - three.js weight has no good lever under current conventions (CDN breaks offline/vendored; no official slim build) - accept; (3) INFO - a bundler would collapse 81 scripts to 1-3, but AGENTS.md pins the no-bundler import-map architecture and the measured load shows no waterfall - documented trade-off, not debt.
- Blocking issues: none.
- Changed files: production/build-size-audit.md (NEW - full artifact with tables, budgets, advisories, verification commands).
- Verification evidence: build.mjs exit 0 immediately before measurement; recursive dist/ walk (file-level sizes in this entry); zlib gzip per file over the whole tree (raw 1,864.3 KB -> 449.6 KB; vendor 290.3 / src 131.4 / assets 27.1 KB); Playwright load profile on clean navigation (86 resources, parallel, 622 ms max end, 890 ms domReady); the 5 fetch-initiated resources are exactly the five assets/*.glb (assetLoader.js).
- Next owner or decision: I198 ST-198 gate G8.5 (cgs-gate-check): advisory phase gate over the I187-I196 arc.
- Result: DONE. ST-197 closed. Next I198 ST-198 (gate G8.5).
